Global Contenders & Defenders 
Momentum reversal, Value bounce 
     
   
 
 
Weak Momentum stocks and Value bounced last month 
Globally, stocks which had been demonstrating weak momentum outperformed in 
October, while stocks which had been demonstrating positive momentum fell.  While this 
momentum reversal contributed negatively to the performance of the Contenders, the 
valuation component contributed positively.  During the month, the Global Contenders 
(-3.2%) underperformed the Defenders (+4.0%) by -7.2% and the MSCI AC World index 
(-1.7%) by -1.5%, reducing the 12-month performance spread to 8.4%.  In Asia last 
month, the Asia Pac Contenders (-6.2%) outperformed the Defenders (-8.1%) by 1.9% 
and the MSCI APxJ Index by 0.7%, extending the 12-month performance spread to 45.7%. 
Three new Global Contenders 
The new Global Contenders this month are Bajaj Auto, Microsoft, and NVIDIA.  The 
longest standing Contender is Citigroup (25 months).  The other Global Contenders are 
Hana Financial, United Microelectronics, Wiwynn, Standard Bank, AerCap, Anheuser-
Busch, Bayer, BNP Paribas, Mitsui O.S.K. Lines, Alphabet-A, Bunge, Cummins, CVS Health 
Corp, Gen Digital, GM, Halliburton, Jabil, KLA, Live Nation, Magna International, Micron 
Tech, Royalty Pharma-A, Seagate Tech, State Street, Steel Dynamics, and Vertiv. 
Five new Asia Pac Contenders 
The new Asia Pacific Contenders are China Citic Bank-H, China Merchants Bank-H, 
PSBC-H, Tal Education-ADR, and Yangzijiang Shipbuilding.  The longest standing 
Contender is Hana Financial (18 months).  The other Asia Pacific Contenders are 
Agricultural Bank-H, Bank of China-H, Bank of Comm.-H, CCB-H, Coway, ICBC-H, KB 
Financial, Korean Airlines, Nanya Tech, PICC-H, PTT, S-Oil, Thai Oil, and Wistron.

Global Implied Allocation 
The Global Implied Allocation indicates whether the combination of earnings and price 
momentum is net positive on stocks in each region, country and sector. 
Our quadrant analysis shown on the previous pages illustrates the various combinations 
of above and below average earnings momentum, and above and below average price 
momentum.  For a particular region, country or sector, if there are more stocks in the 
top-right quadrant (with above average earnings and price momentum) than in the 
bottom-left quadrant (with below average earnings and price momentum) the Implied 
Allocation to that region, country or sector will be positive.  For example, if 46% of the 
stocks in the analysis are USA stocks in the top-right quadrant and 39% are USA stocks 
in the bottom-left quadrant, the Implied Allocation towards the USA would be 7% (the 
difference). The arrows indicate the change in country weight in the last three months. 
 
 
Chart 10: Quadrant Analysis – Implied Country Allocation 
The model is most overweight Japan and Taiwan at country level

Global Contenders & Defenders | 04 November 2026 
 
 
 
How To Read The Charts 
The World seems reasonably efficient in that stock prices generally reflect the 
consensus view.  Stock-price relatives tend to change when expectations change. This 
Contenders & Defenders document shows graphically how changes to consensus 
forecasts relate to stock performance. As well as earnings momentum, the charts 
indicate value in terms of PE relatives. 
Prospective EPS Relative (earnings line) 
The blue Prospective EPS line indicates the direction and magnitude of earnings 
revisions relative to those of the region. Revisions to earnings over the last 3 months 
are ranked from 1 to 100 (1 = biggest downgrade, 50 = median, 100 = biggest upgrade) 
and this EPS Revision Rank is noted next to each chart. 
Price Relative (price line) 
The Price Relative (the solid black line) indicates how the stock has performed relative to 
the region and is based to 100 at the latest point on the chart. 
The relationship between the Price Relative and the Prospective EPS Relative indicates 
the PE relative. If the Price Relative is above the EPS Relative, the stock is on an above-
market PE, while a price line below the earnings line indicates a below-market PE. More 
specifically, dividing the value of the price line by that of the earnings line results in the 
PE relative. For example, if the price line is at 100 and the earnings line is at 80, the PE 
relative is 1.25 (100/80). This enables the reader to see the historical prospective PE of 
the stock relative to the region over time. 
 
The Best Chart 
• 
Prospective EPS Relative is rising (EPS Revision Rank is near 100). 
• 
Price Relative is rising (Price Momentum Rank is near 100). 
• 
Price Relative is below the EPS Relative (PE Rank is near 100). 
A stock with these three characteristics will have a Contenders Rank near 100. 
 
The Worst Possible Chart 
• 
Prospective EPS Relative is falling (EPS Revision Rank is near 1). 
• 
Price Relative is falling (Price Momentum Rank is near 1). 
• 
Price Relative is above the EPS Relative (PE Rank is near 1). 
A stock with these three characteristics will have a Contenders Rank near 1.

Table 6: Rank Definitions 
Rank Definitions 
Rank 
Description 
Contenders Rank 
The companies have been ranked according to attractiveness based on a 
combination of earnings momentum, price momentum, and valuation. 
(100 = Lowest PE stock with strong Earnings Momentum and Price Momentum, 1 = 
Highest PE stocks with low Earnings Momentum and Price Momentum) 
--- 
 
EPS Revision Rank 
The companies have been ranked according to the size of the change in the 
consensus EPS forecast in the last 3 months. 
(100 = Largest EPS Upgrades, 1 = Largest EPS Downgrades) 
--- 
 
Price Momentum Rank 
The companies have been ranked according to their Price Momentum, based on 
month end prices. 
(100 = Highest Price Momentum, 1 = Lowest Price Momentum). 
--- 
 
PE Rank 
The companies have been ranked according the prospective PE. 
(100 = Lowest PE, 1 = Highest PE) 
---

Month End Price

 Global Quant Strategy Publications 
Global Wave 
Our regime Indicator.  The Global Wave quantifies multi-year trends in global economic 
activity and major turning points in the cycle.  The direction of the global cycle 
influences equity market performance, regional allocation, country allocation, style 
allocation, and stock selection. The Bunkers are stocks with style characteristics for a 
downturn and the Boosters are stocks for an upturn. 
Triple Momentum Allocator 
Monthly report highlighting the benefits of combining three types of momentum, namely 
Earnings Momentum, Price Momentum, and News Momentum.  Popular uses include 1) 
identifying stocks with the strongest/weakest Triple Momentum, 2) using Triple 
Momentum for Allocation (regions/sectors/countries), 3) monitoring changes in Triple 
Momentum to highlight when signals are fading and when new opportunities are 
emerging, and 4) monitoring time series in Triple Momentum to show which sectors and 
region-sectors have been in vogue at any point in time in the past. 
Global Positioning in Stocks 
Our Positioning analysis monitors the overweights and underweights of active long-only 
funds globally (AUM $38 trillion) by aggregating active positions by stock, sector, country, 
and region.  Additionally, “Equity Flow” indicates which stocks, sectors, countries, and 
regions long-only funds are buying and selling (dollar value of stocks bought and sold each 
month).  Also, the challenge is to determine which crowded trades may outperform and 
which may face performance hurdles.  We combine our positioning analysis with Triple 
Momentum to highlight which crowded positions have positive catalysts “Crowded 
Positives” and which have negative catalysts “Crowded Negatives”.  Similarly, we screen 
for under-owned stocks with negative momentum “Under-owned Negatives” and under-
owned stocks with positive momentum “Under-owned Positives”.  There is a Global version 
(Global Positioning in stocks) and an Asia version (Pacific Positioning).  
Quant View of Global Themes 
In this regular report, we quantitatively determine which stocks have exposure to various 
Investment Themes (AI, Robotics, Space, Luxury Lifestyle, etc.).  For each theme, we 
analyze long-only positioning, valuation, and trends in Triple Momentum for 1) the 
Theme in aggregate, and 2) each stock within each Theme.  We refresh the analysis 
regularly to include emerging Investment Themes. 
High Frequency Monitor 
A weekly report monitoring high frequency data to help equity investors track short-term 
changes in market-moving data in order to swiftly identify hot topics and turning points. 
Topics include performance, earnings revisions, news, tactical indicators, commodities, 
rates, valuation, geographic revenue exposure, Triple Momentum, and Global Investment 
Themes. 
Global Contenders & Defenders 
A stock selection model based on inexpensive momentum.  This model has been running 
live since 2002.  For each stock, this report graphically shows how consensus earnings 
expectations have changed over decades versus the market, and whether recent 
earnings revisions appear to have been reflected in relative share price performance. 
Popular ways of using this analysis include 1) reviewing the Contenders (buys) and 
Defenders (Sells) lists, 2) perusing individual stock charts, 3) monitoring region, country 
and sector themes using the “quadrant charts”, and 4) reviewing stock rankings.  Includes 
some regional and country versions of the analysis. 

 
 
Global Contenders & Defenders | 04 November 2026
103
 
 
Quantessential Style 
Style rotation.  Different investment styles outperform at various stages of the cycle.  
This investment style analysis relates performance of seven Quantessential Styles 
(Value, Growth, Quality, Risk, Momentum, Dividend and Size) to trends in global macro 
data.  Popular uses include 1) when to buy “Deep Value”, “Growthiest of Growth”, and 
other styles, 2) identifying stocks with various style characteristics, 3) monitoring style 
performance, and 4) explaining out/under-performance due to style tilts.  The is a Global 
version (Global Quantessential Style) and an Asia Pac version (Asia Pac Quantessential 
Style). 
Global Earnings Revision Ratio 
Shows trends in earnings expectations by region, country, and sector.  An analysis of 
Asia countries and sectors is included. 
Quant Panorama 
Summary report.  In this monthly report, we present all our views and recent research on 
regional allocation, country and sector allocation, style allocation, stock selection, and 
much more.  One-stop shopping.  Various versions include “Global Quant Panorama”, 
“Asia Pac Quant Panorama”, “Japan Quant Panorama”, and “Australia Quant Panorama”. 
Global Performance Monitor 
Monthly report analyzing the performance of global regions, countries, sectors and 
identifying the major contributors to global equity market performance.  Useful for 
report writing. 
Global Fund Performance Monitor 
A monthly report enabling investors to monitor how the competition is performing.  We 
analyze the relative returns of large long-only equity funds to understand how returns 
have been affected by fund characteristics.  Funds are grouped by Region, Investment 
Style, Active/Passive, Active Share Ratio, Funds Under Management, Stock Size, Number 
of Stocks, and Portfolio Turnover.   
Global Focus Point 
Brief comments on new research and topical investment issues.  Examples include “The 
Most Important Stocks in the World”, “Tariffs Impact”,  “Narrow Breadth could Persist”, 
and “Ten reasons Investors Underperform”
